Being forced to worship the idols, and refusing to comply, he was beaten severely with rods, to the point that his inner organs were exposed. After being beaten, he was led into the temple of the idols, and again compelled to offer them worship. But he, calling upon Christ and breathing upon the idols, broke them into pieces and reduced them to powder. He was then cast into prison, and seven days later was brought out and presented to Maximus. Again he was urged to sacrifice, and, refusing to comply, he had his ankles pierced with large nails. He was bound to an untamed horse with chains, and having been dragged for twenty-four miles outside the city of the Tyaneans, he rendered up his spirit.
ON THE SAME DAY. 179 The Contest of the holy apostles of the seventy, Olympas, Rhodion, Sosipater, Tertius, Erastus, and Quartus.
Of these holy apostles, Olympas and Rhodion followed the holy apostle Peter, and both were beheaded in the city of Rome by Nero. Sosipater, whom the holy apostle Paul mentions in his Epistle to the Romans, became bishop of Iconium and ended his life in peace. Tertius, whose services the holy apostle Paul used in writing the Epistle to the Romans, was named the second bishop of Iconium after Sosipater and migrated to the Lord. Erastus, whom the same apostle also mentions in the same Epistle, was first the steward of the Church of Jerusalem, and afterwards was made bishop of Paneas, where he died in peace. Finally, Quartus, having become bishop of the city of Berytus, and having suffered much for the faith and converted many of the Hellenes to the Lord, finished his life.
ON THE SAME DAY. 180 The Contest of the holy martyrs Menas, Victor, Vincent, and Stephanis.
The holy great martyr Menas lived under the Emperor Maximian, and was from the land of the Egyptians; he was a soldier under Numerius in the city of Cotyaeum in Phrygia. When it was known that he was a Christian, he was beaten severely and lacerated. After this, he was burned with fire and dragged over sharp caltrops; finally, he received his end by the sword. The holy Victor reached his zenith under the Emperor Antoninus and the duke Sebastian in Italy. Having been seized and forced to deny Christ, and being unable to be persuaded by any means, his finger joints were dislocated and he was thrown into a furnace; then, having consumed a lethal poison which had been offered to him by a magician, and having suffered no harm whatsoever, he converted the magician to Christ. Finally, having had his eyes gouged out and his head...
cut off. But Saint Stephanis, seeing these things and having called the saint blessed, was seized; and being bound to two palm trees that had been bent toward each other, when they were released, she was rent in two. And Saint Vincent, arrested on the same day as a Christian in the city of Augusta, was condemned by the prefect of that city and departed this life.
Eodem die. B 
Commemoration of our holy Father Theodore, superior of the most celebrated monastery of the Studites.
This man was born in Constantinople during the reign of Constantine Caballinus, and having been instructed in all sciences, during the reign of Irene he withdrew to the monastery of Saccudion, where he was made a monk by his uncle Plato, and was ordained a priest by the patriarch Tarasius. When he was thirty years of age, he was made superior of the monastery under him. But since he could not endure the emperor’s adultery, he was relegated to Thessalonica; afterwards, being recalled by the empress Irene on account of the raids which the Saracens were making, he migrated to the royal city and undertook the governing of the monastery of Stoudios. But by the emperor Nicephorus, who perished in Bulgaria, he was for the same reason forced to change his abode again. Finally, on account of the holy icons, he was relegated again by Leo the Iconoclast; and when he had bravely undergone many struggles for piety and had written hymns to God, in extreme old age he departed to the Lord.
EODEM MENSE. C 
Die duodecima.
Commemoration of our holy Father Martin, bishop of Francia.
He was from Rome during the reign of the emperor Julian, and served first as a military commander. But when he saw the vast multitude of the enemy prepared for battle, he was seized with vehement fear. But when he had covered a naked pauper with a portion of the cloak which he was wearing, that same night the Lord appeared to him, clothed in the garment of the pauper, saying: "Do not fear; for just as you did not despise me when I was naked, so I also will be with you, and you shall obtain victory over your enemies." Having therefore conquered, and having been kindly received by the emperor, he gave thanks to God and became a monk. After seven years, when he had attained an understanding of all Holy Scripture through assiduous meditation, he was ordained bishop of Francia. Furthermore, he performed many miracles: among others, when he chanced upon a usurer who was preventing a certain dead man from being buried, saying that he owed him a certain sum of money, while the wife of the dead man contended that all the money had been paid to him, the saint, having poured forth prayers to God, recalled the dead man to life and convicted the slanderer of lying. Finally, having performed many other miracles, he departed this life.
On the same day
Commemoration of our holy Father John the Almsgiver, patriarch of Alexandria.
He was a Cypriot by birth, the son of Epiphanius, who was then the governor of the island. After his wife, whom he had married by the authority of his parents, and the children with whom he had been blessed, had died, he turned all his care to this one end: to please God. Thus, because of the splendor of his life, the people of Alexandria petitioned the Emperor Heraclius that he be made patriarch of Alexandria through the canonical imposition of hands. Having spent many years in that pontificate and performed innumerable miracles—providing abundantly for the needy whatever was necessary—because of his great almsgiving, he deservedly bore the name of "the Almsgiver." At length, having become venerable to all, as the book written about him shows at length, and having converted many of the infidels to the Lord, in a ripe old age he left the earth and flew joyfully to heaven.
IN THE SAME MONTH. THE THIRTEENTH DAY.
184 Commemoration of the exile of our holy Father John Chrysostom.
The commemoration of the exile of Chrysostom is celebrated now, and not that of his falling asleep, for the reason that the latter falls at the same time at which the Exaltation of the precious Cross is commemorated. He was born in the city of Antioch in Syria. Therefore, from the beginning of his life, he acquired a great love for letters and in a short time attained the entire learning of the Greeks and Christians. On account of his virtue, he was chosen by Meletius, the patriarch of Antioch, into the order of clerics, and by Flavian he was ordained deacon and presbyter. But when he had expounded the entire holy Scripture and Nectarius, the patriarch of Constantinople, had departed this life, by the suffrage of the bishops and the command of the Emperor Arcadius, he was summoned from Antioch and established as the high priest of the royal city. But when he rebuked greed, he clashed with the Empress Eudoxia, and, being unjustly exiled by her to the town of Cucusus in Armenia, he died there.
On the same day
185 The contest of the holy martyrs Miles, bishop, and his disciples Ebora, pope [priest], and Seboas, deacon.
These were from Persia. But the holy Miles served formerly in the ranks of war; afterwards, having renounced the duty of service, and having dedicated himself wholly to the service of God, he was ordained bishop in that city of Persia where the prophet Daniel saw visions. But having been driven from there by the infidels, he came to Jerusalem, then to Alexandria to the blessed Anthony; finally, having returned to Persia, he was pierced in the heart by the sword by the ruler Mistophares and his brother, because he had refused to sacrifice to the Sun and to the Fire; to whom, while he was still breathing, he predicted that on the following day the ruler and his brother would mutually
fall by each other’s wounds, at that same hour. Being more vehemently moved by this, they beat his disciples with clubs for so long that they surrendered their souls to the Lord. On the following day, according to the saint’s prediction, the brothers, having been driven to frenzy, slew one another with mutual wounds.
Eodem die.
The contest of the holy martyrs Antoninus, Nicephorus, Germanus, and their companions.
Antoninus the martyr was now advanced in age; but Nicephorus and Germanus were youths. Having been apprehended by the ruler of the region, since they would not sacrifice to idols, but rather confessed Christ, they were beheaded in the city of Caesarea. A certain virgin, Manetho by name, from Scythopolis, was also apprehended and led naked through the entire city; then, having been tortured in various places by the most grievous punishments, and having been snatched from them all by the benefit of God, escaping unharmed, she performed many miracles and converted many of the infidels to the Lord, being instructed by the word of God. Finally, being condemned to the fire, she entered an intensely burning furnace, praying and giving thanks; and having persevered there in prayer for many hours, she finally delivered her sacred spirit into the hands of the living God.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The fourteenth day.
The falling asleep of our holy Father Hypatius the Wonderworker, Bishop of Gangra.
He was one of the three hundred and eighteen Fathers who came together at the Council of Nicaea, under Emperor Constantine the Great, against the delirious Arius. He indeed performed many miracles; but that one is especially memorable, when a dragon of portentous size had crept into the imperial treasury and was driving everyone away from the entrance, he was summoned by the emperor, and having poured forth prayers, he killed it. For he ordered a pyre to be lit in the forum, and he led the dragon out from the treasury—it was clinging with its teeth to the tip of the rod that Hypatius was holding, and following him—and cast it into the fire. Hence the emperor ordered an image of the saint to be accurately painted at the entrance of the treasury itself. But Hypatius, having returned to his own Church, was killed by the snares of the Novatian heretics; for, a stone having been thrown at his head by a certain woman, he was freed from the prison of this life and flew away to the heavenly kingdom.
Eodem die.
Commemoration of the holy apostle Philip, one of the twelve apostles.
Philip, one of the twelve apostles, born at Bethsaida in Galilee, after the Lord’s ascension, having performed many miracles, under the emperor Trajan, came to Hierapolis, together with his daughters, and Mariamne...
his sister, and Bartholomew the apostle, teaching the word of Christ, and persuading the unbelievers to turn away from the deception of the idols; for they worshipped a viper as a god, among whom was Nicanora, the wife of the proconsul, who was the one tasked with punishing them. Then Bartholomew and Philip were hung by the head from the wall. While Philip was hanging and praying, suddenly the earth opened and swallowed the godless ones, together with the proconsul, the viper, and its priests. Then by the grace of Christ, all were brought up, except the proconsul and the viper. Bartholomew and Mariamne were also freed from their bonds. But Philip was perfected.
OF THE SAME MONTH. The fifteenth day.
The contest of the holy confessors Gurias, Samonas, and Abibus, who underwent martyrdom in the city of Edessa.
These existed under Diocletian the emperor and Antoninus the commander. And having been informed against, as those who were ministering to the Christians kept in custody, Gurias and Samonas, who were from the same city and were in the order of the priesthood, were brought before the commander; and not being persuaded to sacrifice to the idols, each of them was hung by one hand for five hours. Then, having been taken down, and persisting in the confession, they were again hung by the feet. Then both were cast into a dark pit, and after this their heads were cut off. But holy Abibus, who was a deacon, underwent martyrdom under Licinius, having been informed against that, as he went around the villages, he read the holy scriptures to the unbelievers, to confirm them. Whence, by the order of the lawless and impious emperor, being cast into a furnace kindled with much brushwood, he was perfected with joy.
On the same day.
The contest of the holy martyr Demetrius, who underwent martyrdom at Dabudenum.
The martyr Demetrius lived under the emperors Maximian and Maximinus, from a village called Dabudenum. Having been informed against by the idolaters as a Christian, he was seized by the governor Publius. And being questioned, he proclaimed with boldness his faith in Christ our God. And being assisted by the Holy Spirit, he taught those who were standing by concerning the incarnation of our Lord Jesus Christ, and his ineffable dispensation, and his entire goodness and philanthropy; then he spoke of the deceptive worship of the idols, and that they are deaf, as are those who worship them. Wherefore, having moved the governor to anger and wrath, he was perfected by having his head cut off with a sword, having received an heavenly crown from Christ, for which he had longed and underwent martyrdom; and his precious relic was buried by the faithful and pious men, putting forth healings upon those who approach it.
On the same day: The Slaughter of Saint James, the Brother of Saint John the Theologian.
James the Apostle was one of the twelve apostles, and was the brother of Saint John the Theologian, the son of Zebedee from Galilee. The Lord, seeing them with Zebedee their father by the Sea of Gennesaret, mending their nets (for they were fishermen), called them; and immediately, leaving everything and their father, they followed Him and gave themselves over to His discipline. But after the Ascension of the Lord and the Falling Asleep of the Theotokos, John went to Ephesus, discharging the office of preaching there; but James, leaving Jerusalem, traversed the twelve tribes of Israel, preaching our Lord Jesus Christ everywhere. Wherefore, having been seized by Herod the Tetrarch in Caesarea, a city of Palestine, and by his order beheaded with a sword, he flew to heaven; where he reigns with Christ the Lord his Teacher, and our most merciful God.
OF THE SAME MONTH. The sixteenth day.
Commemoration of the Holy Apostle and Evangelist Matthew.
Matthew the Apostle was of the number of the twelve apostles; he was a tax collector, and the brother of James who is surnamed Alphaeus. The Lord, having caught sight of him sitting at the custom-house, called him to follow Him; and immediately he followed, and He enrolled him in the number of His disciples. Eight years after the Ascension of Christ, by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, he wrote the holy Gospel designated by his name, and handed it over to the holy Church of Jerusalem. Then, having traversed many provinces of the Gentiles, disseminating the word of Christ, he penetrated even to the anthropophagi. Among them, having endured many torments, and having finally appointed and left behind Plato as bishop there, he proceeded to Hierapolis of Syria, where, having imbued many with the Christian faith and baptized them, he ended his life.
On the same day: The Contest of the Holy Martyr Barlaam.
Barlaam the martyr was born in Antioch of Syria; being an old man in age, he was led to the prefect on account of his confession of Christ. And because he refused to sacrifice to idols, he was most severely beaten with bull-hide thongs; then, his nails having been pulled out, being brought again to the altar, since his hand was spread out by force, he was compelled to receive fire along with incense. For the prefect thought that, if he were to cast the coals along with the incense onto the altar, he would be seen to have offered sacrifice to the gods. But he stood constant and immutable, holding his right hand harder than any bronze or iron, until the flesh beneath, consumed by the fire, fell to the ground, his hand having been perforated; while in the meantime he himself kept his face turned away from the altar, and preferred to have his hand burned.
or to cast incense upon the altar. And thus, with a noble and firm spirit, he delivered up his blessed soul to the Lord.
NOVEMBER.
On the same seventeenth day.
Commemoration of our holy Father Gregory, Bishop of Neocaesarea, the Wonderworker.
He flourished under the emperor Aurelian, the son of Greek parents, but he himself became a Christian and, being taught the mystery of the faith through a vision by the most holy Theotokos and by John the Theologian, he also received the gift of working wonders, and was called the Wonderworker, performing many miracles, from among which are these. For having gone to Alexandria, and having been accused by a harlot, he sent a demon into her; but a little later he also healed her. Then, although he was not present in Amasea, nevertheless, on account of his virtue, he was ordained bishop by Phaidimos of Amasea. Having caused a great stone to walk, he baptized the temple-warden of the idols. And when two brothers were fighting over a lake, he dried it up; and, having planted his staff, he stayed a river, which also grew into a great tree. And having performed ten thousand other wonders, when he was about to depart to the Lord, he gave thanks to God that he had left as many Christians in his city as he had found unbelievers, and thus he was perfected.
On the same eighteenth day.
Contest of the holy martyr Plato.
Plato the martyr was from Ancyra in Galatia, a brother of the holy martyr Antiochus. Because of his confession in Christ, he was seized by the prefect Agrippinus, while he was still young in age; and not being persuaded to sacrifice to the idols, he was beaten by twelve soldiers with rods. And again, having been stretched out upon a red-hot iron bed, he was flailed with cudgels; and his armpits and sides were scorched with red-hot iron spheres, so that a flame mixed with smoke was seen issuing through his nostrils. Then his skin was cut in strips from his head down to his heels. From these, the saint took one strip and cast it into the face of Agrippinus, rebuking his cruelty. After this he was cast into prison, and he fasted for eight days. When those who brought him bread said to him, "Eat," and mocked him, he replied, "The Word of my Christ feeds me." And again he was brought out of custody, and after being tortured, he gladly offered his head to the executioner to be cut off.
On the same day.
Contest of the holy martyr Romanus and the infant with him.
He suffered martyrdom under the impious Maximian. For while on his way, having met the prefect Asclepiades as he was going to the temple of the idols, being kindled with zeal, he...
tried to stop him, saying, "The idols are no gods, but the one God of the Christians is." Whereupon he is struck on the mouth. And being hung up, he is scraped. While being scraped he kept saying that the one God of the Christians is He whom even infants know. And a child was brought by the command of the saint for the confounding of the prefect. And the prefect said to it, "What God do you worship?" And it said, "Him whom the Christians serve and believe in; and this I was taught by my mother." Then, as the child was being beaten in the presence of its mother, it became thirsty and asked for water. The mother said, "Do not drink, my child, of this water, but go, drink of the living water." And thus it was beheaded. But the saint, having had his tongue cut out and yet speaking clearly again, was thrown into prison, and by the command of Maximian was suffocated therein, and received the crown of martyrdom.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The nineteenth day.
The contest of the holy martyr and wonder-worker Aza.
This man was born in the region of the Isaurians and served as a soldier under the impious Licinius. Having abandoned military service, he went off into the deserts and lived a monastic life, occupying himself with prayers and vigils. Hence he became famous for many miracles and healings. But being discovered by hunters who were searching the mountain for game, he was brought to Diocletian. Diocletian therefore sent soldiers who, having seized Aza, brought him before his tribunal, along with a hundred and fifty soldiers who also believed. For by prayer he had brought forth water on the road, which was arid, and having given them to drink, he drew them to the faith. Being enraged at this, Diocletian delivered them all to the prefect Aquilinus, that he might take vengeance upon them. Aza, therefore, being hung up by his hair from a height, was torn by iron claws; then he was cast into a fiery furnace; and when he emerged from it unharmed and untouched, and by that sign led the wife and daughter of the prefect to the faith in Christ, they were all together, along with those soldiers and the saint, beheaded by the angry prefect.
On the same day.
Commemoration of the holy prophet Obadiah.
The holy prophet Obadiah was from the land of Shechem, a region of Palestine. He served Ahab, the king of the Hebrews. When the same Ahab, king of the Hebrews, had sent to the holy, glorious, and great prophet Elijah two captains of fifty to summon him from the mountain to the king, and fire descended from heaven by the prayers of Elijah and consumed them with their company, he sent this third one that he might call him and bring him to him. And when he had come into the presence of the prophet, he fell at his feet and besought him that he might spare him and not let him be consumed by fire. The prophet then descended to the king. But Obadiah, having left the king, followed holy Elijah, and...
becoming his disciple, and having prophesied much, he died and was buried in the tomb of his fathers.
ON THE SAME MONTH, THE TWENTIETH DAY
Certamen of the holy martyr Dasius in the city of Dorostolum.
In this city there was this custom among the Greeks, that thirty days before they celebrated the Saturnalia, they would choose one of the soldiers of notable appearance, whom they intended as a sacrifice to Saturn. Clad in royal attire, they exhorted him to indulge his spirit and satisfy all his desires, given that after the thirtieth day he was to sacrifice himself upon the altar of Saturn. Dasius, the soldier chosen by lot, when faced with his fellow soldiers who were compelling him to this, made use of the best reasoning, and seizing the opportunity afforded to him, said: "Since I must die, it is far better for me, as a Christian, to suffer death for my Christ." Wherefore, by the order of Diocletian and Maximian, he was brought before the prefect’s tribunal, and having been tortured with many torments, he was finally slain by the sword.
ON THE SAME DAY
Certamen of the holy ones who suffered martyrdom in Persia: Nersas, the bishop, and Joseph, his disciple.
These holy ones dwelt in Persia, endowed with singular virtue, and fearing the true God, who made heaven and earth, and the sea, and all things that are in them, and walking according to His commandments. And Nersas, indeed, was the first bishop, and by exhorting the people to piety, he made many disciples, among whom was Joseph, who afterwards, on account of his notable virtues, was ordained bishop by the same Nersas. When therefore they were seized by the atheistic Persians, because they were turning the people from error to virtue, Nersas was then eighty years of age, and Joseph eighty-five. Being brought before the king, they said to him: "O king, if you were to kill us and restore us to life seven times, you would still be unable to draw us away from the love of Christ." When they had said these things, having received the sentence of the sword, they suffered death with a joyful spirit.
ON THE SAME DAY
Certamen of the holy ones John, Saborius, and Isaacius, the bishops, and Dapius and Isaacius, the presbyters.
These holy men also diligently performed their duty as bishops in Persia, teaching the people, and striving to dispel the error of the Persians, demonstrating by vehement discourse that idols, the sun, and fire are by no means gods, but the works and servants
not only of God, but also of men (for they said these things were made for our service, and that there is only one God who produced them), they were denounced to the king. He, seized by great fury and anger, sent soldiers and had them arrested. Bound, they were brought to his tribunal. And having been stripped, they were scourged and lacerated in various ways. But they not only did not deny our Lord Jesus Christ, but also acknowledged Him as the true God and the destroyer of the delusion of the Persians; by order of the tyrant, they were pelted with stones outside the city, together with the holy ascetic Onam.
Eodem die
The Contest of the holy Boethazat the Eunuch, and Sasannis, and the holy men and women with them. B 
These also flourished in the same impious region of the iniquitous Persians, which is given over to idolatry. Boethazat indeed, being a Christian from his first age, was seized; and because he would not sacrifice to the Sun and Fire, he was executed by the order of the king of the Persians, together with the saints, after many torments and tortures. In their number were the holy nuns Thecla and Anna, and many other women, who, having suffered many things for Christ and having more vehemently rebuked the cruelty and folly of the impious king, were beheaded. Similarly, three virgins also died, slain by the sword: from whose blood a fig tree sprouted, which gave a gift of healings to the faithful, driving away every disease and malady, until the atheistic and impious Manichaeans, stimulated by envy on account of the frequency of the divine miracles that were performed there, cut it down.
Eodem die
Commemoration of Saint Gregory the Decapolite.
Gregory the Decapolite was from the Decapolis in the province of Isauria, in the times of the impious Iconoclasts. When he reached adolescence, seeking to avoid the marriage prepared for him by his parents, he became a monk and entered a cave infested by a multitude of demons, by whom he was beaten, but by prayer he finally expelled them. Then, admonished by a vision from God, he left the cave and began to traverse the world. When, therefore, he had come to the city of Syracuse in Sicily, he dwelt in a certain tower of the city wall, where he also performed many miracles. Soon he sought Thessalonica, and then Byzantium. There, some time later, he dwelt near the vision of the divine Simeon, who was imprisoned there for the sake of piety, and having associated with him abundantly and philosophized about the soul, he migrated to the Lord.
EODEM MENSE.
Die vicesima prima.
The Entry of the Deipara, when she was presented to the temple and introduced into the Holy of Holies.
After the admirable [nativity] of the holy Joachim and Anna,
when two years from the birth of the glorious Theotokos and ever-virgin Mary had been completed, Joachim said to his wife Anna: Bring her into the temple of the Lord, as we have promised. And Anna said: Let us wait for the third year, lest, from longing for her parents, she be not directed in the sight of the Lord. And after three years, Joachim said: Let us call the pure maidens of the Hebrews, and let them take lighted lamps, and let the lamps be kept burning; let the child not turn back, and let her mind be captivated in the temple of the Lord. And they did so. Zacharias received the girl and said to her: May the Lord magnify your name. And he seated her upon the step of the altar; and she was fed by an angel until she reached twelve years of age. And when the time of the betrothal arrived, Joseph received her from the high priests in the temple of the Lord.
OF THE SAME MONTH, THE 22ND DAY
The contest of the holy martyrs Marcus and Stephanus.
In the reign of the impious Diocletian, these saints entered into the contest of immortality for Christ. For they were from the city of Antioch in Pisidia; and since they instructed with all freedom those who approached them in the word of truth, and confessed Christ as the true God and Lord of all, they were denounced to the prefect Magnus, and, having been seized, were subjected to torments. And since they could by no means be persuaded to deny Christ, they were bound with another, by surname Marcus, who shone like a star among the holy martyrs called the Calynteni, and were vexed with most bitter tortures of every kind. But persevering in the faith of Christ, they could not be induced to sacrifice to the impure demons; wherefore, by the order of Magnus, their sacred heads were severed, and they were buried in the region of Pisidia, in which they were born.
OF THE SAME MONTH, THE 23RD DAY
The contest of the holy martyrs Archippus, disciple of the apostle Paul, and Philemon the soldier.
These men lived during the reign of Nero. Having become disciples of the holy Paul, they suffered martyrdom in Chonae, a city of Phrygia, which is near Laodicea. For when a festival of the abominable Diana was being held in her temple in Chonae by the superstitious Greeks, these Saints were singing praises to God in the most holy church together with the Christians. Wherefore, an assault having been made upon them by the idolaters, and the others having turned to flight, Archippus, Philemon, and Amphias alone stood their ground. Being bound, they were brought before Androcles, the grain-prefect of Ephesus; and Archippus was beaten with lashes at his command, and because he refused to sacrifice to idols, he was cast into a pit and buried in earth up to his navel,
And having been stabbed all over his body by the pens of schoolboys, he was subsequently stoned to death. Then also the holy Philemon was mercilessly beaten with clubs until he breathed his last.
OF THE SAME MONTH. The twenty-fourth day.
The Contest of the holy martyr Cecilia, and of the saints with her, Valerian and Tiburtius.
Cecilia, a martyr of Rome, born of wealthy parents, was given in marriage to Valerian. She led him to the faith of Christ because of the apparition of an angel, whom she had shown to him. Valerian in turn persuaded his brother Tiburtius to receive baptism. All of these were instructed by Urban, bishop of the city. Moreover, Tiburtius merited such grace that he enjoyed the sight and conversation of angels daily. But when he and Valerian were burying the bodies of the martyrs, they were denounced to the prefect of the city and subjected to torments; they were handed over to Maximus the chamberlain, by whom their venerable heads were cut off. Yet they had previously drawn this same chamberlain, Maximus, to the faith of Christ, who then ended his life, having been tortured by order of the prefect. But Cecilia, having been thrown into a most scorching bath, remained unharmed for three days, and then was beheaded.
On the same day. The Contest of our holy Father, the hieromartyr Sisinnius, bishop of Cyzicus.
The most holy martyr Sisinnius, during the reign of the emperor Diocletian, was bishop of Cyzicus. He taught the multitude of the Greeks the faith of Christ, and converting them from the ancestral superstition of idols to the Lord, he baptized them in His name; he confirmed the faithful, and made them stronger by his exhortations. Furthermore, he visited and cared for those who were arrested for the name of Christ and held in prisons, or subjected to torments, and spurred them on to courageously undergo martyrdom. Wherefore, having been denounced to Diocletian, he was apprehended; first, he was beaten most severely; then, being suspended, his sides were raked. Afterward, he was handed over to prison, and he struggled with hunger and thirst and countless other afflictions. Then, having been brought out of prison, and again urged to deny Christ, because he would not be persuaded, he joyfully received his end by the sword.
On the same day. Commemoration of the holy Gregory, bishop of the city of Agrigentum.
During the reign of Justinian Rhinotmetus, Chariton and his wife Theodote, most hospitable people, lived in the city of Agrigentum. Of them was born this Gregory, whom the bishop received from the font of holy baptism, educated, and made a cleric. When he was eighteen years old, he departed to venerate the holy places...
and he becomes a deacon under Macarius, the bishop of Jerusalem. Then he returned to Byzantium; then to Rome, in which he is also ordained bishop of the Agrigentines. Whence, moved by envy against him, Sabinus and Crescentinus caused him the greatest trouble; however, by the Divine grace, he suppressed their insolence by performing many miracles, and rendered them black, so that unto this day their descendants appear thus. And having received the Church in this manner, and having performed innumerable prodigies, he ended his life in deep old age.
NOVEMBER.
EODEM MENSE.
Die vicesima quinta
The Contest of the Holy Hieromartyr Clement, Bishop of Rome.
Clement, the most wise disciple and successor of the holy apostles, having been saved once from the danger of the sea, and having joined the apostle Peter, was taught by him the truth in Christ, already possessing Greek knowledge. Having become a herald of the Gospel, and having written the Constitutions of the Apostles, he was also established as Bishop of Rome. But, having been seized by the emperor Trajan, he is punished. Then he is sent to Ancyra of Galatia. In which he is even shut up in a certain wall, the door having been blocked with stones. In which he was sustained by legumes alone. And being thus pressed, he made an end of his life. Later, as they say, some believers taking up his precious relics, carried them to Cherson; which, having been cast into the sea by the idolaters, perform innumerable miracles to the glory of Christ even unto this day.
On the same day.
The Contest of the Holy Hieromartyr Peter of Alexandria.
Peter the hieromartyr was bishop of Alexandria under the emperor Maximian. Having been seized, he was about to be beheaded. And hearing this, the heretic Arius, whom the saint had cast out of the Church, came, seeking forgiveness. But the holy Peter did not grant him forgiveness at all, but replied that the Lord had appeared to him by night in the form of a youth, wrapped in a garment rent from head to foot, and to his questioning, "Lord, who has rent your garment?" He had said: "Arius; whom do not admit to communion; for Arius shall not obtain forgiveness either in this age or in the age to come." Now the rent garment signified the division brought into the holy Trinity by the blasphemy of Arius. Having said these things, and having publicly named his successor, and likewise having designated another who was to be sufficient for him, he followed the lictors, and by the command of Maximian was beheaded.
On the same day.
The Contest of the Holy Martyr Mercurius.
The martyr Mercurius lived under the emperors Decius and Valerian, serving in the army of the East, and since he bravely
having occasionally been assisted by an angel in his affairs, was declared a commander of soldiers by the emperors. But, loving Christ, he embraced the Christian faith. When the emperor learned of this, he was vexed. Having seized him, therefore, he ordered him to be tied to four stakes in the ground and cut to pieces limb by limb, and fire to be placed beneath him: which was extinguished by an excess of blood. After this, with a huge stone tied to his neck, he was dragged on a rack, head inverted; and his skin having been stripped off with bronze pincers, he was led bound to Caesarea in Cappadocia: where, after his head was cut off, he rests buried, providing healings to all the faithful to this very day.
On the same day.
The Passion of the holy great martyr Catherine.
Catherine the martyr was from Alexandria, daughter of a certain rich and noble prince, most excellent in beauty and talent. Since she had learned Greek literature, she devoted herself also to philosophical sciences, and was skilled in the languages of all nations. But when at one time a festive day was being celebrated for the idols by the Greeks, and she saw a multitude of animals being slaughtered, she, feeling distressed by this, went to the Emperor Maximinus, and debated with him, saying: "Why, having abandoned the living God, do you worship lifeless idols?" Maximinus therefore, having detained her, tortured her most bitterly. Then, having summoned fifty orators from every quarter, he said: "Dispute with Catherine, and convict her; for unless you convict her, I shall deliver you all to the flames." But they, seeing that they were overcome, were baptized, and thus were consumed by fire. Catherine, however, was beheaded.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The twenty-sixth day.
Commemoration of our holy Father Alypius.
Our holy Father Alypius struggled during the reign of Heraclius. He was from Adrianopolis in Paphlagonia. But before he was born, his mother saw a dream of this kind: She seemed to herself to carry in her arms a lamb of striking beauty, on whose horns burning tapers were fixed: from which she understood that the boy about to be born would be famous for virtues: which also came to pass. For having been ordained a cleric, and then a deacon, having bidden farewell to the affairs of this world, he ascended a column: and having become a stylite, he stood naked under the open sky, illuminated in the meantime by many miracles, and often attacked by demons. He indeed, for as long as he could, prayed while standing on his feet. But having been struck by a stone by demons, he fell into a disease of the side, with which, after he had labored for thirteen years, he departed from life, being one hundred and eight years old.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The twenty-seventh day.
The Passion of the holy and much-struggling martyr James the Persian.
The much-struggling martyr James was from
Persia, during the reign of Theodosius, the Roman Emperor. Because of the excellence of his spirit and the nobility of his lineage, he was held in the highest honor by the king of the Persians. He was a Christian, and had a Christian wife, and likewise a Christian mother and father; yet for the honor of the king, having abjured Christ, he worshipped the Sun and the Fire as if they were gods. When his mother and wife learned of this, they were affected with grief and induced him by their letters to return to the Christian faith. Wherefore, being discovered by the king, and arrested by his command, he suffered a most bitter death. For his limbs and parts of his body were one by one cut off with a sword, until only his torso remained with his head and belly, while in the meantime he offered thanks to God at every single dismemberment. At last, by the order of the same king, his head was cut off with a sword.
IN THE SAME MONTH. The twenty-eighth day.
The Contest of the holy martyr Stephen the Younger, and his companions Peter and Andrew.
Constantine, surnamed Caballinus, an heretical emperor and adversary of the holy images, when he learned that these holy men, Stephen, Peter, and Andrew, neglecting his command, were worshipping the images and calling him a heretic, ordered them to be arrested and first tortured most severely. Then he ordered the holy Stephen to be bound with a rope and dragged through the middle of the city. While he was being dragged, he was struck on the head by a certain man with a large wooden beam, and his head was split in two, and he died, and was buried in the place where he now lies. Similarly, the holy Andrew was also bound and dragged, and departed from this life. But the holy Peter was first thrown into prison, then brought out and beaten with whips for so long that he delivered his spirit to God.
On the same day.
The Contest of the saints who suffered martyrdom for the holy images with the holy Stephen the Younger.
There were then many orthodox soldiers who, renouncing this life, became monks. The lawless emperor punished these men with the harshest torments and killed them. For indeed, he gouged out the eyes of one, named Basil, and as he was still speaking of the veneration of the holy images, he struck him with his heel and ruptured his intestines: then, having banished him, he compelled him to depart this life. Another was placed on the rack, his nose was cut off, and he was exiled to Cherson: whence, consulting for his own safety by flight, he rushed off into Khazaria, where he became a bishop, and later died. Another by the name of Stephen, cast into Sogdia, having been of benefit to many there, reached the end of his life. Likewise, two men named Gregory, with many others, were compelled to leave their native soil, and died. But John, an ex-legatarius, was expelled to Daphnusia, and at fixed times, by the emperor's command, was beaten most frequently, and his life was brought to completion.
On the Same Day
A 218 The Martyrdom of the Holy Martyr Irenarchus.
Irenarchus, the martyr from Sebaste, from his youth performed the office of a lictor to punish the holy martyrs. But when he saw Christian women, dragged to interrogation by Maximus, the prefect of Sebaste, in the times of the Emperor Diocletian, showing themselves far braver than the female sex could bear, and confounding the tyrant with the miracles published by them, he was illuminated by the divine grace of Christ. Having taken courage, he openly confessed Christ and also proclaimed himself a Christian. And first, indeed, by the order of the prefect, he was thrown into a lake; then, when he had escaped from it unharmed, he was cast into a burning furnace; from which, having also emerged without any harm, he was beheaded, and his spirit indeed flew to the heavens; but his remains lie buried in the earth.
OF THE SAME MONTH. The Twenty-Ninth Day.
219 Commemoration of our Holy Father John Damascene.
He flourished in the city of Damascus under the emperors Leo the Isaurian and his son Constantine, distinguished by birth, nobility, and faith, born of a father significant in virtues; and he attained all Greek discipline and also the divine Scriptures. But for the sake of faith in Christ, having abandoned his patrimonial wealth, he became a monk together with Cosmas, whose many hymns exist, which are sung on holy feasts, and especially in the great week of holy Pascha. But the holy John, with a great abundance of words and weight of opinions, and by the authority of holy Scripture, confounding the heresy of the Iconomachs, often punished and vexed by heretical emperors with prison and hardships, died in an excellent confession, and was buried by his disciple.
On the Same Day
D 220 The Martyrdom of the Holy Martyr Philomenus.
Philomenus the martyr, under the Emperor Aurelian, from the region of Lycaonia, exercising trade, was engaged in transporting grain to Ancyra in Galatia. But because he was a Christian, having been denounced to the prefect of Ancyra, he was brought to his tribunal. And when he professed Christ to be the God and Lord of all, first indeed, having his feet and hands pierced with iron nails, he was raised on the rack and lacerated with scourges; then, taken down from the wood, he was cast into a burning furnace: but being affected by no harm, by the power of God, and having his feet, hands, and head pierced again with nails, he was forced to cover thirty stadia in a run, until, exhausted by the running and the labor, he surrendered his blessed soul to God, for whom he underwent martyrdom.
NOVEMBER.
OF THE SAME MONTH. The Thirtieth Day.
The Passion of the holy and all-praised apostle Andrew, brother of the great Peter.
He, being the true brother of the great apostle Peter, after the ascension of our Lord Jesus Christ, preached throughout the entire maritime coast of Bithynia, Pontus, Thrace, and Scythia. After this, he went to the great Sebastopolis, which is intersected by two rivers, the Psarus and the Phasis, among whose inhabitants are the lower Ethiopians. Finally, he journeyed to Patras of Achaia in the Peloponnese, where he was crucified by the proconsul Aegeatas; and thus he met his death. His sacred relics were at that time deposited in Patras of Achaia; but later, by the command of the Emperor Constantine, they were transported by Saint Artemius, together with those of the holy apostles Luke and Timothy, to Constantinople, and were laid beneath the holy table of the holy apostles, emitting the grace of healings.
SECOND PART OF THE MENOLOGION OF THE GREEKS. FROM THE MONTH OF DECEMBER TO FEBRUARY.
MONTH OF DECEMBER. The First Day.
Commemoration of the holy and glorious prophet Nahum.
The great and glorious prophet Nahum was of the tribe of Simeon, from the region of Judaea. Being devout, he received from God the gift of prophecy; and he prophesied against the city of Nineveh after the prophet Jonah, saying that Nineveh was to be destroyed by fresh waters and by fire. And so it came to pass; for by the command of God, because of their sins, the lake surrounding the city of Nineveh was, by a great earthquake, made to submerge it; and fire coming from the desert utterly consumed what remained of it. After having prophesied all these things, he died, and was honorably buried by his kinsfolk in his own land. When he died, he was forty-five years of age. He had a round chin, a thin face, and fine hair. He was also somewhat bald, and had an elongated head.
On the same day:
The passion of the holy martyr Ananias.
Ananias the martyr was from the region of Persia, a layman.
but also neatly and elegantly to write letters in that language. On this account I congratulate you, as it is fitting to congratulate one’s friends on their success and good deeds. I also advise and exhort you not to desist from such distinguished beginnings, nor to betray the good hope of your noble parents; but by long-suffering and patience in labor, may you reach the summit of that glory for which you strive. The new year, which we are soon to enter, God willing, I pray may be most fortunate for you. And since according to ancient custom it is the practice to send gifts around the Kalends of January, as a small token I send you this golden tetrastich of Gregory of Nazianzus:
Work without speech is better than a word without deeds.
No one has ever been exalted by words alone,
But deeds lift up many without words;
For grace is not of the speaker, but of the doer.
Farewell, dearest head.
Rome, December 27, 1661.
PARS PRIMA OF THE MENOLOGION OF THE GREEKS, FROM THE MONTH OF SEPTEMBER TO NOVEMBER
1. Here now, O observer, look rightly,
An excellent work from excellent things:
God’s most beautiful work, striking the mind with awe,
A work rightly delighting every creature.
For He Himself above, as God and Lord,
Having adorned the heavens with the dances of the stars,
Which He extended like a skin by His word,
Illuminates the world with all-wise providence.
But he who portrays this in his ways,
King of the whole earth, sun of the purple,
Basil, child of the swaddling clothes,
Most excellent in both: in triumphs and in words;
As if creating a second heaven in this book,
Extended from skins, as nature requires,
Bearing like luminaries beautiful figures:
First, that of the God-man Word Himself;
Then of the Mother who gave birth without seed;
Of wise men, prophets, angels, archangels; D 
It gladdens the whole mind of the orthodox,
And delights every vision with pleasantness.
But those whom he portrayed with colors,
May he find all of them as helpers in his affairs,
As collaborators in his endeavors, as comrades in battle.
Restrainers of depraved affections, healers of diseases; in the judgment, however, especially before the Lord, fervent mediators, procurers of the supernal Glory, hidden ones, and of the divine sovereignty.
IN THE MONTH OF SEPTEMBER
ON THE FIRST DAY
The Beginning of the Indiction.
The Church of God celebrates the feast of the Indiction, having received this from the ancients, because it was thought to be the beginning of time from this day. For the Romans called the beginning and the definition an "indiction." Therefore, the first day of the month of September is the definition and beginning of the whole year. And since Christ our God, wishing to bless the beginning of time, or rather the whole of time which He Himself created before the ages, entered into the synagogue of the Jews, and there was delivered to Him the book of the prophet Isaiah; and having opened it, He found the place where it was written: "The Spirit of the Lord is upon me; therefore He has anointed me; He has sent me to preach the gospel to the poor, to preach remission to the captives, and sight to the blind, to call the acceptable year of the Lord." And having returned the book to the minister, He sat down. From that time forth, He granted to us Christians this holy feast, which we, celebrating with eagerness, render thanks to Him.
On the same day.
Memory of our holy Father Simeon the Stylite.
Holy Simeon was from Antioch; and while he was yet a youth, he was commanded by his parents to herd sheep. Having stopped one time because of the winter from herding the sheep, he went away to the church of God in order to pray. And by coincidence, he heard the holy Gospel being read: "Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven." And he abandoned his parents and everything. And he went away to the laura, called the mandra, and he became a monk in the reign of Emperor Leo the Great. Where, having first struggled, and in all ways having surpassed all the monks in every sort of virtue, and having withered his body through continence—for he fasted for forty consecutive days, standing for twenty, and sitting for the remaining twenty, because of the fatigue—he afterwards ascended a pillar; and having performed ten thousand miracles, and having become great to all men, and having baptized many of the infidels, and having served God for forty-seven years, he died in peace.
On the same day.
The Dormition of Joshua the son of Nun.
This Joshua was indeed the son of Nun, successor
a brave and military man. For he took Jericho, a city of the foreigners, and crossed the river Jordan on dry ground together with the people and the Ark of God. He also saw Michael, the prince of the celestial host, holding a sword in his hands; and recognizing him at once, he cast down his own weapons and fell at his feet, saying, "What do you command your servant?" And while he was fighting against the foreigners, and the sun was setting, he, being possessed by a desire to defeat them, placed his greatest hope in God and said, "Let the sun stand still." And immediately the sun stood still and did not set until he had turned the enemies of God to flight in a great victory. Having been a general to the people, and having led them well out of the desert, and having brought them into the land of promise, which is Jerusalem, and having judged the people of Israel for twenty-seven years, he died and was honorably buried in that same land by his own people.
On the same day.
The Contest of the holy forty virgins, and Ammon their teacher.
The multitude of these forty virgins and martyrs came from Adrianopolis in Macedonia. They had as a teacher of the faith Ammon the deacon, from whom they learned those things pertaining to the kingdom of heaven; for this reason they were seized by the ruler of that same city. And they were tortured in many ways because they refused to worship idols. Then they were brought to Beroea to sacrifice. There they prayed to God, and the priest of the idols was suspended in the air, and falling, he died. After this, the holy Ammon was suspended, and his sides were rasped, and he was compelled to put on a burning helmet upon his bare head. Then he was sent, along with the virgins, to the tyrant Licinius; and he pronounced a sentence of death against them. And ten of them were perfected by fire; eight were beheaded along with their teacher; ten were killed, pierced by the sword through the mouth and heart; six were cut into pieces; and the final six were extinguished having burning iron placed into their mouths.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The second day.
The Contest of the holy great martyr Mamas.
Mamas, the most renowned martyr, flourished under Aurelian the Caesar of the Romans. Left an orphan after his parents perished in prison for the sake of Christ, he was raised by a certain pious matron of the senatorial rank. When he reached the age of maturity, he was compelled by the idolaters to sacrifice to idols. Being unable to be persuaded, by the divine angel he was taken up into the mountain, where he was fed by the milk of a fawn and continued his life alone, praying to God. But after some time, when his fame had spread far and wide, he was seized again and compelled by the ruler of Caesarea to worship idols. Rejecting this, he was cast into the fire and emerged unharmed, and
was sentenced to the beasts, from which, having received no harm, he was finally pierced through with a spear, and his entrails were poured out upon the earth from the very grievous wound; these he carried with his own hands as he went out of the city of Caesarea, and with thanksgiving surrendered his holy and blessed soul to the Lord God; and he was buried in the same city.
Eodem die.
Memory of our holy Father John, Patriarch of Constantinople, surnamed the Faster.
Our holy Father John, surnamed the Faster, was born in Constantinople. He lived during the times of the emperors Justin, Tiberius, and Maurice. B He first practiced the art of a sculptor, but later, on account of his many and excellent virtues, he was named patriarch of that great city. He was a lover of the poor, merciful, and compassionate. He also performed many miracles; for by his prayers he put to flight barbarian tribes from Macedonia, and delivered the city from a great plague, and healed many who were sick, and cured those possessed by demons; and he wrote prayers most useful to the soul, in the manner of the great John Chrysostom. When he had governed his people holily and piously, and had guided the holy Church most excellently for thirteen years and five months, having reached a ripe old age, he surrendered his blessed and holy soul to the Lord in peace, and was buried, leaving behind as an example to all men his life adorned with virtues and his other noble deeds.
IN THE SAME MONTH.
The third day.
Contest of the holy Hieromartyr Anthimus, Bishop of Nicomedia.
Anthimus the holy martyr was bishop of Nicomedia in the times of Diocletian and Maximian. On account of the persecution stirred up against the Christians, he had hidden himself in the recesses of a certain mountain; betrayed to Maximian and arrested by his command, he was first struck on the neck with stones. Then his ankles were pierced with red-hot irons. Afterwards, he was placed upon sharp potsherds, beaten with thorn rods, and had red-hot iron shoes fitted upon his feet up to his thighs. After this he was bound to a wheel and burned with torches. But while the holy man was being tormented so cruelly, divine angels arrived, who, just as they assuaged his punishments and torments, invisibly struck the executioners who were torturing him. Finally, by the sentence of Maximian, his head was cut off with a sword; which head lies in Nicomedia and does many other miracles, and indeed even miraculously puts forth hair.
Eodem die.
Contest of the holy virgin and martyr Basilissa.
Basilissa the martyr was in the times of Diocletian.
She had her dwelling near the city of Nicomedia, and was in her ninth year. Being denounced as a Christian, she was brought before Alexander the governor. And having confessed Christ with boldness, she was first struck on the face, and having been stripped, she was scourged. Then her ankles were pierced. And having been hung head downwards upon a beam, bound with chains, she was tortured with the smoke of pitch, sulfur, and lead. And after this she was thrown into the fire, and delivered to two lions, and having remained unharmed, she persuaded the governor to believe in Christ. And he indeed, living well, ended his life in peace. But she, having gone out of the city, and being thirsty, prayed. And water flowed forth from the earth, and she drank. Then standing upon a rock, and having prayed, she died. There she was buried, and, through the waters flowing forth from the earth, she wonderfully produces healings for all the faithful each day.
On the same day. Contest of the holy hieromartyr Aristion, bishop of Alexandria.
The holy martyr Aristion was bishop of Alexandria. He taught with boldness, and preached the incarnate advent of our Lord Jesus Christ, and His eternal kingdom, and furthermore the blessedness and joy of the kingdom of heaven; and that the glory, dignities, and riches of men here are subject to corruption, and vanish away; whereas those goods are incorruptible, and that life is immortal. Teaching these things, and persuading many of the Greeks and converting them to God and baptizing them, he was arrested by the prefect of Alexandria. And having confessed Christ, he was delivered to the fire. And thus in the furnace, like those three holy children, praising and magnifying God, he received the blessed and longed-for end, having obtained the crown of incorruptible life. And his precious remains were indeed committed to the earth. But his holy soul, ascending into the heavens, rejoices with the saints.
OF THE SAME MONTH. The fourth day. Contest of the holy hieromartyr Babylas, patriarch of Antioch, and the children with him.
The holy hieromartyr of Christ, Babylas, was patriarch of the great Antioch, in the time of Numerian, emperor of the Romans. When Numerian himself wished to enter the temple of God, Babylas met him at the gate of the church and forbade him from entering, because he was a worshiper of idols. Wherefore the emperor, being put to shame and provoked to violent anger, ordered him to be brought before him the next day; and when he could not induce him to deny Christ, but rather was himself rebuked by him, he commanded a heavy weight of iron to be placed upon his neck and for him to be led through the middle of the city. Then he said to him: "By the gods, Babylas, this yoke becomes you."
To which Babylas replied: "So it becomes me, O emperor, just as the diadem becomes you." Then Numerianus passed sentence against him and against the boys who were being instructed by him. Then the soldiers took them and slaughtered them. And thus the saint was buried by pious Christians outside the city of Antioch, together with the shackles surrounding him, which he had worn for the sake of Christ.
On the same day B 
The Contest of the holy martyrs Theodore, Oceanus, Amianus, and Julian.
These holy martyrs flourished under Maximian the emperor. Being seized and interrogated, they confessed Christ the Lord. And for this reason, having been suspended, they were flayed to the bone. Then they were cast into a bath that had been heated intensely, and sealed with the emperor’s seal so that they might not escape from it but be forced to perish within it. But an angel of the Lord led them out unharmed, while the doors were bolted and sealed. Again, having been captured, they were led outside the city into a desert place to be cruelly put to death. Having requested an hour of prayer from the executioners, and having obtained their request, they stood and prayed at length to God, first for the establishment of the whole world and for the tranquility of the holy Churches; then they gave thanks to Him that they were deemed worthy to undergo martyrdom for His sake. And so, with all their limbs cut off and their legs broken, they were cast into the fire, and thus they were perfected.
On the same day
The Contest of the holy martyr Hermione, daughter of the holy apostle Philip.
The holy martyr Hermione lived under Trajan the emperor; she was the daughter of the apostle Philip. She also had a sister named Eutyches. And the two arrived at Ephesus to venerate John the Theologian, but did not find him, for he had already been moved. Trajan the emperor also came to Ephesus for the war against the Persians, and found the name of the holy Hermione to be great among the pious; for she was gifted with the prophetic spirit. Having seized her and tried to force her to deny Christ, when he could not persuade her, he ordered her to be struck on the face. The Lord appeared to her and strengthened her to endure the tortures with fortitude. After this, she was released by Trajan because she had prophesied for him that he would triumph over the Persians. But after Trajan, his son-in-law and successor, Hadrian, seized her again. And when he compelled her to deny Christ and to sacrifice to the idols, and failed to persuade her, he ordered her to be beheaded. The hands of the executioners having withered, they themselves also believed in Christ, and were beheaded together with her.
On the same day 
Memory of the holy Moses the legislator.
Moses the prophet and legislator, born of a Hebrew father, was born in Egypt. But because there was a command of Pharaoh the king...
of Egypt that the female infants of the Hebrews should be spared, but the males should be put to death, his mother placed Moses in a wicker basket and threw him into the river. And the daughter of Pharaoh went down to the river to bathe. And seeing the basket, and the child crying inside it, she adopted him as her son. And she instructed him in all the wisdom of the Egyptians. When he had grown up, God appeared to him and commanded him to lead the people of the Hebrews out of Egypt. Having therefore led the people out, and having performed many signs and wonders, and having parted the sea, and having crossed on dry land, and having struck a rock with his rod and caused water to gush out from it, and having led them for forty years, he died one thousand four hundred and eighty-five years before the resurrection of Christ. And he was buried on a certain mountain in the desert by Michael, the great captain of the host of God.
ON THE SAME MONTH. The Fifth Day.
Memory of the holy prophet Zacharias, the father of John the Baptist.
Zacharias the priest, the father of John the Forerunner, was himself also the son of a priest. Having entered the temple to burn incense, he saw an angel of God standing near the altar of incense, announcing to him that his son John the Baptist would be conceived and born to him; and because he did not believe his voice, he was rendered deaf and dumb, until the bonds of his tongue were loosed at the birth of John. But the mother of John, hearing that Herod, the king of the Jews, was planning to kill the infants in Bethlehem, wishing to kill our Lord Jesus Christ along with them, then, six months after his conception, hid him in a cave beyond the Jordan. Herod, having sought him and not finding the child, ordered his father Zacharias to be slaughtered in the middle of the altar, because he had fled there in fear, and that his blood should be shed in the temple. He was slain, therefore, and they cast him down the precipice. His relatives gathered up his relics and placed them in the tomb of his fathers.
On the same day.
The Contest of the holy martyr Abdias.
The holy martyr Abdias, a Persian by nation and a Christian by profession, used every effort to teach everyone, and by his exhortations persuaded them to come to the true faith in our Saviour Jesus Christ. Having been seized for this reason by the prince of the magi, and forced to abjure Christ and worship the sun and fire (for the Persians held these as gods), and not being persuaded, he is struck in the belly and on the loins with rods of harsh pomegranate. And so, from morning until noon, being beaten and tortured, being carried on the shoulders of his executioners, he is taken to his house half-dead; and shortly afterward
he surrendered his holy and blessed soul to the Lord, rejoicing and exulting until his final breath, in that he was deemed worthy to bear witness for the name of our Lord Jesus Christ and to die. After this, some of the Christians took his precious remains and laid them in a tomb.
On the same day: The contest of the holy martyrs Thuthael and his sister Babaea.
The holy and multi-victorious martyr of Christ Thuthael and his sister Babaea, being Christians and confessing with boldness that our Lord Jesus Christ is the true God and Lord of all the world, and Creator and Providence, and turning many of the Greeks from the error of idols and bringing them to the true faith and baptizing them, were seized by the impious idolaters. And having been beaten and tortured much by them, in the end the holy Thuthael was hanged upon a beam, and being bound, he was sawed in half with an iron saw; and thus he surrendered his soul. His sister Babaea, having been stripped first, and beaten severely, and flayed even to the bones, was afterwards struck with a spear at the neck and slaughtered. Both having thus been consummated, those who had been instructed by them in the Christian faith took up their bodies and buried them honorably to the glory of our Lord Jesus Christ.
In the same month, the sixth day: Memory of the miracle wrought at Chonae by the great Archistrategos of God.
In the city now called Chonae, but formerly Colossae, there lived a certain man named Archippus, devout and fearing God. This man, staying by the water which gushed forth through the visitation of the great Archistrategos, and because of the miracles performed through the water, was converting and baptizing many of the unbelievers; he stirred up to envy the Greeks, who could not bear to see those wondrous miracles. Gnashing their teeth, therefore, against the sacred holy site and the holy Archippus, they gathered infinite multitudes, and damming two great rivers and mixing both together, they released them straightway in order to destroy the temple, and the holy site, and Archippus. But when Archippus heard the roaring of the waters, he besought the Archistrategos to help. And he, appearing immediately, struck the rock with the rod which he held in his hand, and he caused the waters to be swallowed up within it. And the place was called Chonae from that time onward.
On the same day: The contest of the holy martyrs Eudoxius, Romulus, and their companions. Eudoxius, the martyr of Christ, was in the time of the Emperor Trajan...
holding the rank of count. Having ten thousand soldiers under him, he was staying in the land of Gaul. Romulus, however, who was the superintendent of the imperial palace, advised Trajan to send someone to Gaul to compel the soldiers—who were Christians—to sacrifice to the gods. When this was done, they refused to sacrifice; and because of this, they were exiled to the city of Melitene in Armenia. Afterwards, Romulus, moved by repentance and having believed in Christ, rebuked Trajan and was beheaded. After these events, when Maximian had obtained the empire and had ordered the Christians to be killed, he sent written commands to the prefect of Melitene to destroy those who worshipped Christ. Whereat, he seized Eudoxius the count and the soldiers with him; and because they refused to worship the idols, he first tormented them with many tortures and eventually beheaded them.
Eodem die
The contest of the holy martyrs Faustus the presbyter, Abibus the deacon, and their companions.
The holy martyrs Faustus and Abibus lived under the Emperor Decius, the former being a presbyter and the latter a deacon of the Church of Alexandria; at which time also Fabian, patriarch of Rome, and Alexander of Jerusalem suffered martyrdom for Christ. Having been detained by the prefect of Alexandria and severely tortured because they did not wish to abandon Christ, they were cast into prison. Being led out again, they were compelled to perform sacrifices to the idols. But they were so far from being moved to do this that they actually rebuked the prefect and scorned the error and vanity of the idols. For this reason, the heads of these holy men—thirteen in number—were cut off. But their holy souls, commended into the hands of Christ our God, intercede incessantly for us Christians, and grant our faithful emperors strength and vigor against their enemies for the glory of Christ, for whom they shed their blood.
EODEM MENSE. Die septima
The contest of the holy martyr Sozon.
The martyr Sozon was born in the region of Lycia, having been a shepherd of sheep. When he once came upon an immense oak near which there was water, he hung up his three arrows and his bow from the tree as a memento of himself; and through the prayers of the holy man, that place received the gift of divine healings. He taught the Greeks and baptized them in the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit. After this, he entered the temple of the idols, tore off the golden hand of the idol, and breaking it into pieces, distributed it to the poor. When the author of this act was sought, and many were being seized, the holy man voluntarily came forward and presented himself to the governor Maximus, confessing the true faith of Christ our God. For this reason, iron soles with sharp nails were fastened to the plants of his feet, and wearing them, he walked about, praising God. Then, being hung from a tree, he was beaten unto death. And his body was cast into...
a furnace of fire.
The same day.
The struggle of the holy martyr Eupsychius.
Eupsychius the martyr was from Caesarea of Cappadocia, under Hadrian the Roman emperor. After the death of his father, he was baptized by the bishop of Caesarea. Thereafter, being denounced by certain persons as a Christian, he was seized by the governor of Caesarea and cast into prison. After this, upon coming out of prison and selling his possessions, he first gave a portion of them to his accusers, as they had become the agents of the kingdom of heaven for him; the rest he distributed to the poor. Being seized again and brought before the governor, he was compelled to sacrifice to idols. Refusing to be persuaded, he was first hung upon a wooden beam and scraped to such a degree that all his inner organs could be seen. Then, being taken down from the wood and struck with a sword, he was perfected, having surrendered his spirit to the Lord.
EODEM MENSE.
The eighth day.
The Nativity of our most holy Lady Theotokos and Virgin Mary.
When the Creator God had fashioned mankind for good works, and so that they might know Him alone and do His will, the devil envied them. First, he persuaded Adam to transgress, having been deceived through his wife, Eve. Then he also persuaded the rest of mankind to abandon God and worship lifeless idols. Having compassion, therefore, on His creation, God gave them the Law and the prophets. But as these things were of no avail, He willed to send His Son and Word to assume the form of a man and to deliver men from the hand of the devil. For this reason, He ordained that she who was to give birth in the flesh to His Son—Mary, the immaculate Theotokos—should be born of holy parents, Joachim and Anna, to whom God had promised, as they prayed, to grant the fruit of the womb. Hence, the present feast is called the Nativity. Celebrating this annually, we give thanks to the Lord our God.
EODEM MENSE.
The ninth day.
Commemoration of the holy Joachim and Anna, the parents of the Theotokos.
In the people of the Jews there was a man named Joachim, a just and wealthy man, and at every feast he would divide his wealth into two parts. One part he gave to the poor, the other he offered to God. But he was childless. On one of the feast days, therefore, he was offering his gifts to God, and because he had no child, he was reproached by a certain Jew, who said to him, "It is not fitting for you to offer gifts to God with us, because you are childless." Joachim was grieved, and did not return to his
house, but went away into the desert, and fasted for forty days, entreating God to grant him the fruit of the womb. Anna meanwhile was praying the same thing in her house. And God, hearing them, sent an angel who promised them the birth of our holy Lady, the Theotokos and ever-virgin Mary. This indeed came to pass, for the salvation of the whole human race.
On the same day B 
The contest of the holy martyr Severianus.
Severianus the martyr was from the city of Sebaste, and was a soldier in the reign of Licinius. When the holy forty martyrs had been arrested and thrust into prison for Christ's sake, he went to them and encouraged them to endure their martyrdom. Having been discovered and seized, he was brought before the tribunal of Lysias the duke. And having openly confessed Christ, and refuted the error, and steadfastly opposed Lysias, he was suspended and scourged with ox-hide thongs, his sides were lacerated, and he was burned with torches. After this, he was suspended from a high wall, bound with ropes, his feet burdened with the weight of massive stones, and his hands tied behind his back. Having been suspended for a long time and suffering under the blows, his nerves being disjointed by the weight of the stones and his limbs dissolved, he meanwhile gave thanks to God and prayed, and thus, having been bitterly tortured, he died, committing his spirit into the hands of God.
ON THE SAME MONTH.
The tenth day.
Commemoration of the holy Baripsabbas.
At the time when the Jews, out of envy, crucified our Lord Jesus Christ, there was a certain man named Jacob who stood by the cross. He, having seen the soldier pierce the side of the Lord with a spear, and that blood and water flowed forth, ran and took a gourd/pot, received the precious blood in it, and hid it secretly. And when he was dying, he left it to two hermits. And so, handed down through the generations, it came into the hands of a certain pious monk named Baripsabbas, who through it performed many healings. But before he died, taking this same holy blood together with the gourd, he journeyed through every city and region, performing many miracles. Certain men who did not fear God, observing this, came to the conclusion that if they killed Baripsabbas, they would possess the gourd with the precious blood and make a great profit from it. Therefore, they set upon him by night and killed him. They indeed took the gourd, but found nothing of what they had hoped for. He, however, thus slaughtered, gave up his spirit.
On the same day D 
The contest of the holy martyrs and sisters Menodora, Nymphodora, and Metrodora.
Menodora, Nymphodora, and Metrodora, sister martyrs, were from the region of Bithynia, in the time of the Emperor Maximian, dwelling in the Pythia, where...
the warm waters emerge. Because they loved Christ, and were teaching the Greeks in His name, they were seized by the ruler of the region. Being questioned, they confessed Christ. For this reason, the eldest sister, Menodora, was beaten first, and her legs were crushed with rods. And thus, praying, she was perfected. The governor commanded her remains to be placed before the other sisters, in order to strike terror into them, and he said to them: "Sacrifice to the gods so that you do not suffer the same things as your sister." But when he did not persuade them, he commanded the executioners to kill them as well. And immediately the executioners, taking iron bars, crushed all their limbs. And thus having completed their martyrdom, they were buried near the warm waters, bestowing healings.
ON THE SAME MONTH. THE ELEVENTH.
The contest of the holy martyrs Diodorus and Didymus.
Diodorus and Didymus, martyrs of Christ, were from the city of Laodicea in Syria. Being Christians, they instructed everyone in the name of the Lord our God, who made heaven and earth, and all things that are in them. And turning many of the faithless, they baptized them. But having been denounced by the impious Greeks to the ruler of Laodicea, they were brought to his tribunal. And being questioned, and confessing themselves to be Christians, they were severely beaten. Having been tortured for a long time, and not denying Christ, but rather bravely exposing the deception of the idols, and reproaching the ruler as lawless and acting evilly, and spitting in his face because he had abandoned the true God and was devoted to false idols, they met their death while giving thanks and praying to Christ in the midst of their torments.
On the same day
 The contest of the holy martyr Ia, who suffered martyrdom in Persia.
The martyr of Christ, Ia, was from a Roman fortress situated near the region of Persia. She was taken captive by Sapor, king of the Persians, along with nine thousand Christians. And having been discovered to worship Christ, she was handed over to the chief magi to be punished. They, having urged her to deny Christ, not only failed to persuade her, but were mocked by her. For this reason she was cast into prison, where she was worn down by hunger for an entire year. Then, having been brought out of prison, she was beaten with rods of impure pomegranate. And again, having been shut away for fifteen months, she was brought to the tribunal of the chief magi. And being bound with thin cords, her hands, thighs, and legs were tightened by a winch until her bones made a cracking sound. And again she was beaten on her loins and belly with cowhide straps. And after these things, giving thanks, she was beheaded by the sword.
On the same day: Commemoration of Saint Theodora of Alexandria
Saint Theodora flourished under the Emperor Zeno. She was of outstanding beauty and the daughter of wealthy parents, and had married a pious man who feared God. A certain wealthy man, stirred by the devil, became inflamed with love for her and, having employed a procuress suitable for such purposes, fulfilled his sin. After the sin was committed, Theodora, repenting of the deed and unable to bear the shame, cast off her woman’s garment and put on the poor attire of a man. She went to a monastery of men, giving herself the name Theodore, while her husband meanwhile lay in perpetual mourning and squalor on her account. She endured such temptations from the demons and undertook such a struggle against them that she not only washed away the stain of her sin but was also deemed worthy by God of the gift of many miracles. The monks, believing her to be a man (for she had taken every care to remain hidden), found her to be a woman by nature when they washed her after her death, and they extolled God.
In the same month, the twelfth day. Contest of the holy Hieromartyr Autonomus
The holy martyr Autonomus lived under the Emperor Diocletian and was a bishop in Italy. Because of the persecution stirred up against the Christians, he left Italy and reached Bithynia, where he was received by a certain Christian named Cornelius. Having built a church in the name of the Archstrategos Michael, and having ordained Cornelius as bishop there, he went to Claudiopolis, where he preached the word of God. However, upon returning to visit Cornelius and officiating the liturgy in the temple of the Archstrategos Michael, he provoked the Greeks to fury, for they bore it ill that Christ was worshipped by him while their idols were neglected.
